## Objective
- Add native value-based `RANGE` window framing to Druid in `/app`.
- The new type must be `WindowFrame.Range`.
- `WindowFrame.Range` must be a public nested class of `WindowFrame`.
- `WindowFrame.Range` must be registered as Jackson subtype `"range"`.
- Multi-key `RANGE` queries must still fail when the work is done.

## Important Details
- `WindowFrame.Range` must have a `@Nullable Double lowerOffset`.
- `WindowFrame.Range` must have a `@Nullable Double upperOffset`.
- `WindowFrame.Range` must have a non-null `ColumnWithDirection orderBy`.
- The required getter for the lower offset is `getLowerOffset()`.
- The required getter for the upper offset is `getUpperOffset()`.
- The required getter for the ordering column is `getOrderBy()`.
- A null offset must mean a positionally unbounded side.
- A non-null offset must be a signed distance in the ordering key's value space.
- The required static factory is `WindowFrame.range(@Nullable Double lowerOffset, @Nullable Double upperOffset, ColumnWithDirection orderBy)`.
- `Range` must not extend `OffsetFrame`.
- `Range` must override `equals` by value.
- `Range` must override `hashCode` by value.
- `Range` must support Jackson serialization.
- `Range` must support Jackson deserialization.
- `FramedOnHeapAggregatable#aggregateAll` must accept `Range`.
- `WindowFramedAggregateProcessor` must accept `Range`.
- `WindowFirstProcessor` must accept `Range`.
- `WindowLastProcessor` must accept `Range`.
- For a non-null ordering key value `v` and a finite offset `o`, a bound must be `v + o` under `ASC`.
- For a non-null ordering key value `v` and a finite offset `o`, a bound must be `v - o` under `DESC`.
- A `Range` frame must be the closed interval between its lower bound and its upper bound.
- Unbounded sides of a `Range` frame must reach the partition edges.
- Unbounded sides of a `Range` frame must include rows whose key is `NULL`.
- When the current row's key is `NULL`, finite bounds must resolve to the edges of the contiguous `NULL` run that holds the row.
- Peers with equal keys must get identical frame bounds.
- Peers with equal keys must get identical output.
- An empty frame must yield the aggregator's zero-row default.
- `FIRST_VALUE` must return `null` on an empty frame.
- `LAST_VALUE` must return `null` on an empty frame.
- A `Range` that covers the whole partition for every row must evaluate the same as `WindowFrame.unbounded()`.
- `LONG` ordering columns must work.
- `FLOAT` ordering columns must work.
- `DOUBLE` ordering columns must work.
- Range resolution must work for every `RowsAndColumns` implementation.
- Timestamp columns reach the native engine as `LONG`.
- An ordering type that cannot do value arithmetic with a finite offset must throw an unchecked `DruidException`.
- The SQL validator must accept offset `RANGE` frames.
- The SQL validator must accept one-sided offset `RANGE` frames.
- The SQL validator must accept offset `RANGE` frames with a `CURRENT ROW` bound.
- Calcite planning must turn offset `RANGE` frames into native frames.
- Offset `RANGE` support must apply only when the window has exactly one `ORDER BY` key.
- The single key must be numeric or a timestamp.
- The bound literal may be a number.
- The bound literal may be a day-time `INTERVAL`.
- A day-time `INTERVAL` must map to its millisecond magnitude as a floating-point offset with no integer truncation.
- An offset `RANGE` frame with finite bounds on a non-numeric and non-interval key must be rejected during validation or planning.
- `WindowFrame` maps the Jackson name `"rows"` to `WindowFrame.Rows`.
- `WindowFrame` maps the Jackson name `"groups"` to `WindowFrame.Groups`.
- `WindowFrame.unbounded()` returns `rows(null, null)`.
- The agent found earlier that `ColumnWithDirection` encodes `ASC` as `ASC(1)`, read through `getDirectionInt()`.
- The agent found earlier that `ColumnWithDirection` encodes `DESC` as `DESC(-1)`, read through `getDirectionInt()`.
- Only `DefaultFramedOnHeapAggregatable` implements `FramedOnHeapAggregatable` in the main code.
- `QueryableIndexRowsAndColumns` serves `as()` through its `AS_MAP`.
- The agent concluded that handling `Range` in `DefaultFramedOnHeapAggregatable` and in the value processors covers every `RowsAndColumns`.
- The agent judged that `compareRows` returns -1 for a null left row, so null keys sort first under `ASC`.
- The agent judged that the sort multiplies `compareRows` by the direction, so null keys sort last under `DESC`.
- The agent judged that `compareRows(null, null)` returns 0, so the `NULL` rows form one peer group.
- The agent judged that `ClusteredGroupPartitioner.computeBoundaries` returns the edges of contiguous runs of equal values.
- Decision: a new class `RangeFrameResolver` in `org.apache.druid.query.operator.window` will compute the peer groups and the frame start and end of each group.
- The reason is that `DefaultFramedOnHeapAggregatable` and `WindowValueProcessorBase.computeFirstOrLastValueFramed` both need the peer groups and their frame intervals.
- Decision: the peer groups come from `computeBoundaries` on the `ORDER BY` column.
- The planned `RangeFrameResolver` constructor takes `RowsAndColumns rac` and `WindowFrame.Range frame`.
- The planned `RangeFrameResolver` keeps the peer group edges in `groupBoundaries`.
- The planned `RangeFrameResolver` keeps one frame start per group in `frameStarts`.
- The planned `RangeFrameResolver` keeps one frame end per group in `frameEnds`.
- Decision: both users of `RangeFrameResolver` will iterate the peer groups directly, without merging adjacent groups that have identical frames.
- The agent judged that such merging is possible but not worth doing.
- Decision: keys are compared as doubles through `accessor.getDouble(row)`.
- The reason is that `getDouble` treats `LONG`, `FLOAT` and `DOUBLE` keys the same way.
- The agent accepted a precision loss for key values beyond 2^53.
- Under `ASC`, the planned frame start is the first row whose value is at least the lower bound.
- Under `ASC`, the planned frame end is the first row whose value is greater than the upper bound, exclusive.
- Under `DESC`, the planned frame start is the first row whose value is at most the lower bound.
- Under `DESC`, the planned frame end is the first row whose value is less than the upper bound, exclusive.
- The planned search covers only the non-null rows, because `getDouble` on a null row gives 0.0.
- Decision: `isEffectivelyUnbounded` treats a `Range` with both offsets null as unbounded.
- Decision: `moveTo` recomputes the whole interval for `Range` frames.
- The reason is that both ends of a `Range` interval can move at the same step.
- `DruidSqlValidator.validateWindow` rejects a `RANGE` frame unless both bounds pass `isUnboundedOrCurrent`.
- The agent judged earlier that this rejection is raised as a `CalciteContextException`.
- The agent judged earlier that `updateBoundsIfNeeded` turns the single bound `RANGE 0 FOLLOWING` into `RANGE BETWEEN CURRENT ROW AND 0 FOLLOWING`.
- The agent read in the parser bytecode that Calcite 1.41 sets the frame type with `createBoolean(true)` for `ROWS`.
- The agent read in the parser bytecode that Calcite 1.41 sets the frame type with `createBoolean(false)` for `RANGE`.
- The agent concluded that Calcite 1.41 cannot parse `GROUPS` frames.
- The agent concluded that a false `isRows()` therefore means `RANGE`.
- `Window.Group` at the Rel level has only the boolean `isRows`.
- Quidem `!error` checks that the squashed stack trace contains the squashed expected text.
- The `qaWin` files expect only `QueryInterruptedException` before `!error`.
- The agent judged that any Druid validation or planning error therefore still matches the `qaWin` expectations.
- The `qaWin` `RANGE` cases the agent printed expect a `QueryInterruptedException` line followed by `!error`.
- The agent found earlier that the `qaWin` `RANGE` cases it printed have several `ORDER BY` keys or none.
- A grep of the `qaWin` files found `RANGE` windows with no `ORDER BY`, such as `OVER (PARTITION BY client_ip RANGE 0 FOLLOWING)`.
- It is unconfirmed whether every `qaWin` `RANGE` case has several `ORDER BY` keys or none.
- Decision: unsupported `RANGE` forms will keep `Order By with RANGE clause currently supports only UNBOUNDED or CURRENT ROW. Use ROWS clause instead.` as the validator rejection message.
- The reason is that the multi-key `RANGE` cases in the `qaWin` quidem files then keep failing.
- Decision: the `qaWin` files are left as recorded.
- Decision: an offset `RANGE` frame with no `ORDER BY` is rejected.
- The reason is that `WindowFrame.range` needs an ordering column.
- Decision: the validator accepts an offset `RANGE` frame only with exactly one `ORDER BY` key.
- Decision: the validator accepts an offset bound literal only if it is numeric or a day-time `INTERVAL`.
- Decision: the key type check runs at planning time in `Windowing.getWindowFrame`.
- The reason is that the row signature `sig` is available there.
- `isLongType` counts `TIMESTAMP` as a long type, so the row signature reports timestamp keys as `LONG`.
- `Windowing.java` builds row frames as `WindowFrame.rows(getBoundAsInteger(group.lowerBound), getBoundAsInteger(group.upperBound))`.
- `Windowing.java` builds group frames as `WindowFrame.groups(getBoundAsInteger(group.lowerBound), getBoundAsInteger(group.upperBound), getOrderingColumNames())`.
- `Windowing.java` has no range branch yet.
- The agent judged that `RANGE` frames with only `UNBOUNDED` or `CURRENT ROW` bounds on `STRING` keys work today through the `groups(...)` frame.
- Decision: `Windowing.WindowGroup.getWindowFrame()` will keep the `groups(...)` path when no bound is a `PRECEDING` or `FOLLOWING` offset.
- Decision: `Windowing.WindowGroup.getWindowFrame()` will emit `WindowFrame.range(...)` only when at least one bound is a `PRECEDING` or `FOLLOWING` offset.
- The reason is that this keeps all existing behavior and tests.
- The agent found earlier that `RexWindowBound` exposes `getOffset()`.
- The agent judged earlier that frame offsets reach `Windowing` as `RexInputRef` entries into `window.constants`.
- The agent judged earlier that the constant is read as `window.constants.get(refIndex - sig.size()).getValue()`.
- Decision: the planned `RANGE` branch will read offsets through a new `getBoundAsDouble` reader that takes `doubleValue()` from the offset constant's `Number`, instead of `getBoundAsInteger`.
- The reason is that `Range` offsets are `Double` values.
- The agent judged earlier that `getBoundAsInteger` keeps only `intValue()`.
- The planned `getBoundAsDouble` returns null for an unbounded bound.
- The planned `getBoundAsDouble` returns 0.0 for a `CURRENT ROW` bound.
- The planned `getBoundAsDouble` returns the negated value for a `PRECEDING` bound.
- The planned `getBoundAsDouble` returns the value as it is for a `FOLLOWING` bound.
- A day-time `INTERVAL` literal holds milliseconds, from `SqlParserUtil.intervalToMillis`.
- A year-month `INTERVAL` literal holds months, from `intervalToMonths`.
- Decision: year-month `INTERVAL` bounds are rejected.
- The agent judged that `RewriteFirstValueLastValueRule` rewrites only `CURRENT ROW` bounds.
- The agent judged that `RewriteFirstValueLastValueRule` does not interfere with offset `RANGE` frames.
- `testUnSupportedRangeBounds` runs `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY dim1 RANGE BETWEEN 3 PRECEDING AND 2 FOLLOWING)`.
- `testUnSupportedRangeBounds` expects the old validator rejection message.
- The agent noted that `dim1` is a `STRING` column.
- The agent judged that this query will now pass, because `ROW_NUMBER` never calls `getWindowFrame()`.
- `WindowFrameTest` checks frame equality with `EqualsVerifier.forClass(...).usingGetClass().verify()`.

29:  * A semantic interface used to aggregate a list of AggregatorFactories across a given set of data
30:  * <p>
31:  * The aggregation specifically happens on-heap and should be used in places where it is known that the data
32:  * set can be worked with entirely on-heap.  There is support for frame definitions, frames aggregate certain

34:  * where a boundary could be based on rows or it could be based on "PEER" groupings.

Now I have the validator picture. Let me now write the WindowFrame.Range class. Key design:

```java
class Range extends OffsetFrame  // wait — no, Range must NOT extend OffsetFrame since offsets are Double
```

Per the requirements: "Range must not extend OffsetFrame" — actually let me re-read: "The required static factory is WindowFrame.range(...)"; "`Range` must not extend `OffsetFrame`." Hmm, wait, actually the summary says Range must not extend OffsetFrame? Let me re-read the summary: "- `Range` must not extend `OffsetFrame`." Yes — because OffsetFrame has Integer offsets. Range has Double offsets. So Range implements WindowFrame directly.

```java
class Range implements WindowFrame
{
  @JsonProperty
  private final Double lowerOffset;
  @JsonProperty
  private final Double upperOffset;
  @JsonProperty
  private final ColumnWithDirection orderBy;

  @JsonCreator
  public Range(
      @JsonProperty("lowerOffset") Double lowerOffset,
      @JsonProperty("upperOffset") Double upperOffset,
      @JsonProperty("orderBy") ColumnWithDirection orderBy)
  {
    this.lowerOffset = lowerOffset;
    this.upperOffset = upperOffset;
    this.orderBy = Preconditions.checkNotNull(orderBy); // or Objects.requireNonNull
  }

  public Double getLowerOffset() { return lowerOffset; }
  public Double getUpperOffset() { return upperOffset; }
  public ColumnWithDirection getOrderBy() { return orderBy; }

  @Override
  public int hashCode() { return Objects.hash(lowerOffset, upperOffset, orderBy); }

  @Override
  public boolean equals(Object obj)
  {
    if (this == obj) return true;
    if (obj == null || getClass() != obj.getClass()) return false;
    Range other = (Range) obj;
    return Objects.equals(lowerOffset, other.lowerOffset)
        && Objects.equals(upperOffset, other.upperOffset)
        && Objects.equals(orderBy, other.orderBy);
  }

  @Override
  public String toString() {...}
}
```

Wait — EqualsVerifier requires... For EqualsVerifier.forClass(Range.class).usingGetClass().verify() — it needs a non-final class or prefab values? Actually EqualsVerifier works on classes with final fields fine. Rows/Groups work. Range with a ColumnWithDirection field: EqualsVerifier will need to create instances of ColumnWithDirection — it can since it has a constructor. But ColumnWithDirection has an enum Direction field. EqualsVerifier handles enums fine. But there's a subtlety: EqualsVerifier may complain about the constructor requiring non-null (if we Preconditions.checkNotNull). Rows and Groups don't have null checks. EqualsVerifier handles null fields fine unless the constructor throws NPE... Actually EqualsVerifier creates objects via reflection and may pass null values to constructor fields? No — EqualsVerifier uses values that are non-null by default ("red" values). Now the resolver. Let me design `RangeFrameResolver`... wait, but where should resolution live? The decision from summary: new class in `org.apache.druid.query.operator.window` package. It needs:
- input: AppendableRowsAndColumns rac (for aggregation path) — but WindowValueProcessorBase uses `ClusteredGroupPartitioner.fromRAC(rac).computeBoundaries(...)`, which takes plain RowsAndColumns. DefaultFramedOnHeapAggregatable.buildIteratorFor takes AppendableRowsAndColumns but only uses rac.numRows() and ClusteredGroupPartitioner.fromRAC(rac). So the resolver should take RowsAndColumns.
- frame: WindowFrame.Range

API:
- `int[] getGroupBoundaries()` — peer group boundaries
- per group g (0..numGroups-1): `int getFrameStart(g)`, `int getFrameEnd(g)` (exclusive). Or an `Iterable<AggInterval>`-like structure.

But AggInterval/Interval are package-private static classes inside DefaultFramedOnHeapAggregatable (in org.apache.druid.query.rowsandcols.semantic). The resolver in org.apache.druid.query.operator.window can't reference them. So the resolver should expose primitives: boundaries + frameStart/frameEnd per group. Then:

- DefaultFramedOnHeapAggregatable builds its Iterable<AggInterval> from those.
- WindowValueProcessorBase.computeFirstOrLastValueFramed uses boundaries + frameStart/frameEnd to pick first/last value.

Design of RangeFrameResolver:

```java
public class RangeFrameResolver
{
  private final int[] groupBoundaries;    // numGroups+1 entries
  private final int[] frameStarts;       // numGroups entries
  private final int[] frameEnds;         // numGroups entries

  public RangeFrameResolver(RowsAndColumns rac, WindowFrame.Range frame) { ... }

  public int getNumGroups() { return groupBoundaries.length - 1; }
  public int getGroupStart(int g) { return groupBoundaries[g]; }
  public int getGroupEnd(int g) { return groupBoundaries[g + 1]; } // exclusive
  public int getFrameStart(int g) { return frameStarts[g]; }
  public int getFrameEnd(int g) { return frameEnds[g]; } // exclusive
}
```

Resolution algorithm:

Given frame: lowerOffset Double (nullable = unbounded), upperOffset Double (nullable = unbounded), orderBy ColumnWithDirection(column, direction).

Data is sorted by the ordering column within the partition (window operator sorts by ordering). Nulls sort first (ASC) or last (DESC) per LongColumnAccessorBase compareRows semantics: null lhs returns -1 → nulls first in ASC; direction multiplies → nulls last in DESC.

Wait — actually, let me double check DefaultNaiveSortMaker to make sure the sort semantics matches accessor.compareRows * direction. From summary: "DefaultNaiveSortMaker uses accessor.compareRows * direction". So data within a partition is sorted such that compareRows(a,b)*directionInt gives ascending order. With nulls: compareRows(null, x) = -1 (null lhs → -1). So in ASC (directionInt=1): null row sorts before x. In DESC (directionInt=-1): -1*-1=+1 → null sorts after x → nulls last. Good.

Also compareRows(null, null) = 0.

Now, the ordering column values are numeric (LONG/FLOAT/DOUBLE — planner enforces). At native level, resolver should defensively check type via accessor.getType().getValueType() is one of LONG/FLOAT/DOUBLE... but wait — if both offsets are null (unbounded), Range ≡ unbounded and no value comparison is needed. The aggregation path calls isEffectivelyUnbounded first, so resolver would only be created for non-unbounded frames. But actually the resolver can handle both-null trivially. For type check: if either offset non-null, require numeric type, else throw DruidException. Since planner already ensures numeric keys, this is defensive.

Type check: ColumnType of the column. What about STRING keys with unbounded Range? getWindowFrame in Windowing: if not isRows and bounds are unbounded/current → groups path... wait no. Let me re-examine: my plan for Windowing.getWindowFrame:

```java
public WindowFrame getWindowFrame()
{
  if (group.lowerBound.isUnbounded() && group.upperBound.isUnbounded()) {
    return WindowFrame.unbounded();
  }
  if (group.isRows) {
    return WindowFrame.rows(...);
  }
  if (isOffsetBound(group.lowerBound) || isOffsetBound(group.upperBound)) {
    // RANGE frame with at least one PRECEDING/FOLLOWING offset: value-based range
    // check exactly one ordering key and numeric type
    final List<RelFieldCollation> fields = group.orderKeys.getFieldCollations();
    if (fields.size() != 1) { throw ... }
    final RelFieldCollation field = fields.get(0);
    ... direction check ...
    final String column = sig.getColumnName(field.getFieldIndex());
    final ColumnType columnType = sig.getColumnType(field.getFieldIndex());
    if (!(columnType.isNumeric() or is long-timestamp)) { throw QueryException SQL_QUERY_UNSUPPORTED }
    return WindowFrame.range(getBoundAsDouble(group.lowerBound), getBoundAsDouble(group.upperBound), new ColumnWithDirection(column, direction));
  }
  return WindowFrame.groups(...);
}
```

This is getting overcomplicated. Simplest coherent semantics (DuckDB-like): when current row key is null:
- bound unbounded → partition edge (0 or numRows)
- bound finite (offset or CURRENT ROW) → null run edge: lower → nullRunStart, upper → nullRunEnd.

And isValidRangeBound:

```java
/**
 * Checks if the given endpoint is valid as a value-based RANGE offset: numeric or day-time interval literal.
 */
private boolean isValidRangeBound(@Nullable SqlNode bound)
{
  if (isUnboundedOrCurrent(bound)) {
    return true;
  }
  if (bound.getKind() != SqlKind.PRECEDING && bound.getKind() != SqlKind.FOLLOWING) {
    return false;
  }
  final SqlNode boundVal = ((SqlCall) bound).operand(0);
  if (boundVal instanceof SqlNumericLiteral) {
    return true;
  }
  if (boundVal instanceof SqlIntervalLiteral) {
    final SqlIntervalLiteral.IntervalValue intervalValue =
        (SqlIntervalLiteral.IntervalValue) ((SqlIntervalLiteral) boundVal).getValue();
    return !intervalValue.getIntervalQualifier().isYearMonth();
  }
  return false;
}
```

Check: SqlIntervalLiteral.IntervalValue has getIntervalQualifier() ✓ (public method). isYearMonth() on SqlIntervalQualifier ✓ exists. SqlNumericLiteral covers INTEGER/DECIMAL/DOUBLE literals ✓ (negative literals: `-1 PRECEDING` → SqlNumericLiteral with negative value ✓).

Hmm — what about `RANGE INTERVAL '1' DAY PRECEDING`? boundVal = SqlIntervalLiteral ✓ day-time → valid. Then planning: RexWindowBound offset RexLiteral value: for interval literal → BigDecimal of millis (from summary: SqlLiteral.getValueAs(BigDecimal) → intervalToMillis). getBoundAsDouble:

```java
private Double getBoundAsDouble(RexWindowBound bound)
{
  if (bound.isUnbounded()) {
    return null;
  }
  if (bound.isCurrentRow()) {
    return 0.0d;
  }
  final RexLiteral constant = window.constants.get(((RexInputRef) bound.getOffset()).getIndex() - sig.size());
  final double value = ((Number) constant.getValue()).doubleValue();
  return bound.isPreceding() ? -value : value;
}
```

Wait — constant.getValue() for interval literal returns BigDecimal (millis) ✓ Number ✓. For numeric literal returns BigDecimal ✓.

```java
public RangeFrameResolver(final RowsAndColumns rac, final WindowFrame.Range frame)
{

  final Column column = rac.findColumn(orderColumn);
  if (column == null) {
    throw DruidException.defensive("Column[%s] does not exist", orderColumn);
  }
  final ColumnAccessor accessor = column.toAccessor();
  final int numRows = accessor.numRows();
  if (frame.getLowerOffset() != null || frame.getUpperOffset() != null) {
    final ColumnType type = accessor.getType();
    if (!type.isNumeric()) {
      throw DruidException.defensive(...);
    }
  }
  groupBoundaries = ClusteredGroupPartitioner.fromRAC(rac).computeBoundaries(ImmutableList.of(orderColumn));
  ...
}
```

Null run detection: isNull(rowNum). But WHERE is the null run? Scan: the first group whose first row isNull → null run [groupBoundaries[g], groupBoundaries[g+1]). But — is compareRows(null, null) == 0 guaranteed for ALL accessors? LongColumnAccessorBase ✓; StringColumnAccessorBase? Float/Double? Probably (null==null → 0 pattern). But safer: don't rely on peer grouping for null detection — compute null run by scanning isNull over sorted data: nulls contiguous ✓ (sorted). Actually simpler: during group iteration, for each group check isNull(groupBoundaries[g]) — the group's first row. If null → null group. Actually — safer approach that doesn't depend on compareRows null semantics for grouping: I use computeBoundaries for GROUPS (needed for outputRows anyway — and the same nulls-in-one-group assumption is ALREADY relied upon by the existing Groups frame path for RANGE/CURRENT ROW semantics with null keys... wait — existing groups frames: `ORDER BY x RANGE CURRENT ROW` — GROUPS frame path — window functions like FIRST_VALUE with string keys + nulls → computeBoundaries groups nulls together → GROUPS logic treats null peer group ✓ existing behavior). So computeBoundaries null-grouping is ALREADY the codebase's peer semantics ✓ reuse it.

BUT the value comparisons (binary search) use getDouble — nulls return 0.0 — MUST exclude nulls from binary search region! Null run location: ASC → [0, nullRunEnd) nulls FIRST; DESC → [nullRunStart, numRows) nulls LAST.

Hmm wait — is that ACTUALLY guaranteed? DefaultNaiveSortMaker: sort comparator = compareRows(a,b) * directionInt. compareRows(null, x) = -1 (null lhs). ASC: null vs x → -1 → null first ✓. DESC: -1 * -1 = +1 → null AFTER x → nulls last ✓. Null vs null: 0 ✓ stable.

So null run: ASC → prefix; DESC → suffix. Detect: scan for first null. If ASC: nullRunStart = 0 (if any nulls, nullRunEnd = count of nulls... wait nulls all contiguous at start ✓ nullRunEnd = idx of first non-null). If DESC: nulls at end: nullRunStart = numRows - nullCount.

Hmm — but scan direction: just find first index f with isNull(f): ASC → nullRun = [0, e) where e = first non-null after f... contiguous ✓. DESC → nullRun = [numRows - k, numRows)... first null at f = numRows - k ✓ nullRun = [f, numRows).

Actually — even simpler: find FIRST null index f (if exists). For ASC: nullRun = [f, e) with e = first idx ≥ f non-null — but if nulls are contiguous prefix, f = 0 (guaranteed? nulls sort before everything in ASC → yes f = 0 if any nulls... UNLESS mixed... sorted → all nulls first → f=0 ✓). For DESC: nulls last → nullRun = [f, numRows) ✓ (f = first null = start of suffix).

But defensive: DON'T assume; verify: check isNull(f..): all nulls contiguous? Just trust the sort ✓ (same trust as computeBoundaries).

Null group vs null run: the null peer group SHOULD equal the null run. I'll use the null run from isNull scan for null-bound resolution (independent of compareRows null semantics), and groupBoundaries for output grouping. For the null row's frame with finite bounds → [nullRunStart, nullRunEnd) = null run — matches null peer group if compareRows(null,null)=0 ✓ consistent.

Now binary searches. Values in non-null region: ASC sorted ascending. Need:

```java
// ASC: first idx in [lo, hi) with getDouble(idx) >= target; hi if none
private static int lowerBoundAsc(ColumnAccessor acc, int lo, int hi, double target)
{
  // binary search
  while (lo < hi) {
    int mid = (lo + hi) >>> 1;
    if (acc.getDouble(mid) < target) lo = mid + 1; else hi = mid;
  }
  return lo;
}
// ASC: first idx with getDouble(idx) > target
private static int upperBoundAsc(...) { ... if (acc.getDouble(mid) <= target) lo = mid+1; else hi = mid; ... }
// DESC region [lo, hi) sorted descending: first idx with val <= target
private static int lowerBoundDesc(...) { ... if (acc.getDouble(mid) > target) lo = mid + 1; else hi = mid; }
// DESC: first idx with val < target
private static int upperBoundDesc(...) { ... if (acc.getDouble(mid) >= target) lo = mid + 1; else hi = mid; }
```

Wait let me verify lowerBoundDesc: region desc-sorted. Want first idx with val <= L. Values: [10, 9, 8, 7], L = 8.5: first val ≤ 8.5 → 8 at idx 2 ✓. Binary: mid val > target → move right (lo = mid+1) since desc prefix has larger values; val ≤ target → hi = mid. ✓.

upperBoundDesc: first idx with val < U: [10,9,8,7], U = 8: first < 8 → 7 idx 3 ✓. mid val ≥ U → lo = mid+1; val < U → hi = mid ✓.

Hmm wait — but these DESC searches are restricted to [0, nullRunStart)... For null current-row handling I use null-run rules, and for non-null groups the searches stay in non-null region ✓.

Now per-group resolution:

```java
final boolean asc = frame.getOrderBy().getDirection() == ColumnWithDirection.Direction.ASC; // or directionInt == 1
final Double lowerOffset = frame.getLowerOffset();
final Double upperOffset = frame.getUpperOffset();

for (int g = 0; g < numGroups; g++) {
  final int groupStart = groupBoundaries[g], groupEnd = groupBoundaries[g+1];
  final int row = groupStart; // first row of group (representative)
  if (accessor.isNull(row)) {
    frameStarts[g] = lowerOffset == null ? 0 : nullRunStart;
    frameEnds[g] = upperOffset == null ? numRows : nullRunEnd;
  } else {
    final double v = accessor.getDouble(row);
    if (asc) {
      frameStarts[g] = lowerOffset == null ? 0 : lowerBoundAsc(accessor, nullRunEnd, numRows, v + lowerOffset);
      frameEnds[g] = upperOffset == null ? numRows : upperBoundAsc(accessor, nullRunEnd, numRows, v + upperOffset);
    } else {
      frameStarts[g] = lowerOffset == null ? 0 : lowerBoundDesc(accessor, 0, nullRunStart, v - lowerOffset);
      frameEnds[g] = upperOffset == null ? numRows : upperBoundDesc(accessor, 0, nullRunStart, v - upperOffset);
    }
  }
}
```

Wait — nullRunStart for ASC = 0, nullRunEnd = e. lowerBoundAsc search region [nullRunEnd... but nullRunEnd = e = first non-null. If no nulls: e = 0 → region [0, numRows) ✓. If all nulls... numGroups... if all rows null: nullRun = [0, numRows), non-null region empty; groups: 1 group (nulls); null row → null rules ✓ never searches.

Hmm wait — subtle: what if no nulls in ASC? nullRunEnd = 0 → search [0, numRows) ✓. If nulls exist ASC: nullRunEnd = e ✓ search [e, numRows) — but WAIT: should the search region be [nullRunEnd, numRows) or [frameStart-independent]? Yes [nullRunEnd, numRows) ✓ nulls excluded.

BUT — what about ASC lowerBound when L ≤ ... hmm what if L < some null... nulls = -inf conceptually: {x ≥ L}: nulls ≥ L? -inf ≥ L false (L finite) → excluded ✓ search from nullRunEnd ✓. What if L is very negative, e.g. L = -1e300? -inf ≥ L still false → nulls excluded ✓. Consistent: value semantics treats null as -inf, never ≥ any finite L, always ≤ any finite U... 

WAIT — ASC frameEnd: {x ≤ U} includes nulls (-inf ≤ U ✓ ALWAYS)! Frame = {L ≤ x ≤ U}: nulls in iff L = -inf (unbounded). frameStart rule: L finite → search [nullRunEnd, numRows) → excludes nulls ✓; L unbounded → frameStart = 0 → includes nulls ✓. frameEnd: U finite → upperBoundAsc([nullRunEnd, numRows)) — hmm — {x ≤ U} non-null part ends at upperBoundAsc; nulls are BEFORE nullRunEnd ≤ frameEnd?? nulls in frame only if frameStart = 0 → frame [0, frameEnd): frameEnd = upperBoundAsc ≥ nullRunEnd? If U < min non-null: all non-nulls > U → frameEnd = nullRunEnd → frame = [0, nullRunEnd) = nulls only ✓✓ CORRECT ({x ≤ U} = nulls, since all non-null > U... wait {x: -inf ≤ x ≤ U} with U < minVal: nulls (-inf ≤ U ✓) ✓ + non-nulls ≤ U (none) ✓ = nulls only ✓ PERFECT).

If U ≥ min non-null: frameEnd = first x > U ≥ index of min ≥ nullRunEnd ✓ frame includes nulls when frameStart = 0 ✓ and excludes when frameStart > 0 (frameStart ≥ nullRunEnd > nulls' indices) ✓✓ ALL CONSISTENT.

DESC nulls = +inf: {x: U ≤ x ≤ L}: nulls in iff U = +inf (unbounded upper). frameEnd: U unbounded → numRows → includes nulls ✓; U finite → upperBoundDesc region [0, nullRunStart) → frame ≤ nullRunStart → excludes nulls ✓. frameStart: L unbounded → 0 ✓; L finite → lowerBoundDesc [0, nullRunStart) ✓. Null run: ASC → nulls at start [0, nullRunEnd); DESC → [nullRunStart, numRows). Compute:

```java
final boolean asc = frame.getOrderBy().getDirection() == ColumnWithDirection.Direction.ASC;
int nullRunStart = 0, nullRunEnd = 0;
// find null run
if (asc) {
  while (nullRunEnd < numRows && accessor.isNull(nullRunEnd)) nullRunEnd++;
  // nullRunStart = 0
} else {
  nullRunStart = numRows;
  while (nullRunStart > 0 && accessor.isNull(nullRunStart - 1)) nullRunStart--;
  // nullRunEnd = numRows
}
```

Wait but for ASC, nullRunStart = 0 (nulls first) — but only if there are nulls at all. If no nulls, nullRunEnd = 0 → null run empty [0, 0) ✓. DESC: nullRunStart = numRows when no nulls → run [numRows, numRows) ✓.

Null-run edge values needed:
- ASC: nullRunEnd (used as search region start), nullRunStart = 0.
- DESC: nullRunStart (search region end = nullRunStart), nullRunEnd = numRows.

Universal rules: lower finite → nullRunStart; lower unbounded → 0. upper finite → nullRunEnd; upper unbounded → numRows.

For null current row:
- frameStart = lower == null ? 0 : nullRunStart  (ASC: 0 == nullRunStart anyway)
- frameEnd = upper == null ? numRows : nullRunEnd

Hmm wait — for ASC non-null rows, search region [nullRunEnd, numRows); L unbounded → frameStart = 0 (includes nulls). But hold on — {x: x ≥ -inf} = EVERYTHING including nulls ✓ 0 ✓.

For non-null row:
```java
final double v = accessor.getDouble(groupStart);
if (asc) {
  frameStart = lower == null ? 0 : lowerBoundAsc(accessor, nullRunEnd, numRows, v + lower);
  frameEnd = upper == null ? numRows : upperBoundAsc(accessor, nullRunEnd, numRows, v + upper);
} else {
  frameStart = lower == null ? 0 : lowerBoundDesc(accessor, 0, nullRunStart, v - lower);
  frameEnd = upper == null ? numRows : upperBoundDesc(accessor, 0, nullRunStart, v - upper);
}
```

Binary searches:

```java
private static int lowerBoundAsc(ColumnAccessor accessor, int lo, int hi, double target)
{
  // first index in [lo, hi) with value >= target; hi if none
  while (lo < hi) {
    final int mid = (lo + hi) >>> 1;
    if (accessor.getDouble(mid) < target) {
      lo = mid + 1;
    } else {
      hi = mid;
    }
  }
  return lo;
}
```

upperBoundAsc: first with value > target: `if (accessor.getDouble(mid) <= target) lo = mid+1; else hi = mid;`
lowerBoundDesc: first with value <= target: `if (accessor.getDouble(mid) > target) lo = mid+1; else hi = mid;`
upperBoundDesc: first with value < target: `if (accessor.getDouble(mid) >= target) lo = mid+1; else hi = mid;`

All in non-null region ✓ (getDouble returns garbage 0.0 for nulls, excluded).

Hmm — wait, one important check: DESC search region [0, nullRunStart): region values sorted desc ✓; but lowerBoundDesc finds "first index with val ≤ L". Since desc sorted, prefix has largest values. First index where val ≤ L: all before it have val > L ✓. ✓.

Now potential issue: the null run detection assumes nulls are ONLY at the boundaries (all nulls contiguous). With mixed... sorted guarantee ✓.

BUT — one more: what if the ORDER BY column direction passed in Range disagrees with the actual sort? The window operator machinery sorts by ColumnWithDirection from Windowing (same orderBy object flows into the frame) ✓ consistent by construction. Native query users construct both sort and frame — their responsibility.

Now — the value semantics check: nulls as -inf/+inf. My non-null row rules: ASC frameStart (L finite): search [nullRunEnd, numRows) — nulls excluded even if L very negative ✓ (-inf ≥ L false). ASC frameEnd (U finite): search [nullRunEnd, numRows) → frameEnd ≥ nullRunEnd always... wait NO: upperBoundAsc(nullRunEnd, numRows, U) ≥ nullRunEnd ✓ always ≥ nullRunEnd. Hmm — but {x ≤ U} includes nulls conceptually — but frameStart only includes nulls when frameStart = 0 < nullRunEnd → frame [0, frameEnd) with frameEnd ≥ nullRunEnd → nulls fully in frame ✓; frameStart ≥ nullRunEnd → nulls fully out ✓. NEVER partial null inclusion ✓✓ good — nulls can't be partially in frame since they're contiguous at the edge and frameEnd ≥ nullRunEnd.

DESC: frameStart (L finite) = lowerBoundDesc(0, nullRunStart, L) ≤ nullRunStart → nulls (at [nullRunStart, numRows)) excluded when frameStart... wait frame ≥ frameStart: nulls in frame iff frameEnd > nullRunStart → frameEnd = upperBoundDesc ≤ nullRunStart (U finite) → nulls excluded ✓; U unbounded → frameEnd = numRows → nulls included ✓; L unbounded → frameStart = 0. ✓✓ consistent.

3. buildRangeIteratorFor:

```java
private static Iterable<AggInterval> buildRangeIteratorFor(AppendableRowsAndColumns rac, WindowFrame.Range frame)
{
  final RangeFrameResolver resolver = new RangeFrameResolver(rac, frame);
  final int numGroups = resolver.getNumGroups();
  return () -> new Iterator<AggInterval>()
  {
    int currentGroup = 0;

    @Override
    public boolean hasNext() { return currentGroup < numGroups; }

    @Override
    public AggInterval next()
    {
      if (!hasNext()) {
        throw new IllegalStateException();
      }
      final int g = currentGroup++;
      return new AggInterval(
          Interval.of(resolver.getGroupStart(g), resolver.getGroupEnd(g)),
          Interval.of(resolver.getFrameStart(g), resolver.getFrameEnd(g))
      );
    }
  };
}
```

5. Windowing.getWindowFrame + buildRangeFrame + getBoundAsDouble.

Let me write:

```java
public WindowFrame getWindowFrame()
{
  if (group.lowerBound.isUnbounded() && group.upperBound.isUnbounded()) {
    return WindowFrame.unbounded();
  }
  if (group.isRows) {
    return WindowFrame.rows(getBoundAsInteger(group.lowerBound), getBoundAsInteger(group.upperBound));
  }
  if (isOffsetBound(group.lowerBound) || isOffsetBound(group.upperBound)) {
    // RANGE clause with at least one offset-based bound
    return buildRangeFrame();
  }
  /* Right now we support GROUPS based framing in the native layer;
   * but the SQL layer doesn't accept that as of now.
   */
  return WindowFrame.groups(getBoundAsInteger(group.lowerBound), getBoundAsInteger(group.upperBound), getOrderingColumNames());
}

private WindowFrame buildRangeFrame()
{
  final List<RelFieldCollation> fields = group.orderKeys.getFieldCollations();
  if (fields.size() != 1) {
    throw new QueryException(
        QueryException.SQL_QUERY_UNSUPPORTED_ERROR_CODE,
        StringUtils.format(
            "Order By with RANGE clause with offset-based bounds requires exactly one ORDER BY key, found [%d]",
            fields.size()
        ),
        null,
        null
    );
  }
  final RelFieldCollation field = fields.get(0);
  final ColumnWithDirection.Direction direction;
  switch (field.direction) {
    case ASCENDING: direction = ColumnWithDirection.Direction.ASC; break;
    case DESCENDING: direction = ColumnWithDirection.Direction.DESC; break;
    default:
      throw new QueryException(...same pattern as getOrdering...);
  }
  final String columnName = sig.getColumnName(field.getFieldIndex());
  final ColumnType columnType = sig.getColumnType(field.getFieldIndex()).orElse(null);
  if (columnType == null || !columnType.isNumeric()) {
    throw new QueryException(
        QueryException.SQL_QUERY_UNSUPPORTED_ERROR_CODE,
        StringUtils.format(
            "Order By with RANGE clause with offset-based bounds requires a numeric or timestamp key, found column [%s] of type [%s]",
            columnName, columnType
        ),
        null, null
    );
  }
  return WindowFrame.range(
      getBoundAsDouble(group.lowerBound),
      getBoundAsDouble(group.upperBound),
      new ColumnWithDirection(columnName, direction)
  );
}
```

Wait — "Cannot handle ordering with direction[%s]" — the getOrdering() method handles direction. Hmm — can I reuse getOrdering()? getOrdering returns ArrayList<ColumnWithDirection> for all order keys with sig names ✓. But it doesn't type-check. I could do:

```java
final List<RelFieldCollation> fields = group.orderKeys.getFieldCollations();
if (fields.size() != 1) throw ...
final RelFieldCollation field = fields.get(0);
final ColumnWithDirection columnWithDirection = ... direction switch ...
```

```java
private WindowFrame buildRangeFrame()
{
  final List<RelFieldCollation> fields = group.orderKeys.getFieldCollations();
  if (fields.size() != 1) {
    throw new QueryException(SQL_QUERY_UNSUPPORTED_ERROR_CODE, "...", null, null);
  }
  final RelFieldCollation field = fields.get(0);
  final String columnName = sig.getColumnName(field.getFieldIndex());
  final ColumnType columnType = sig.getColumnType(field.getFieldIndex()).orElse(null);
  if (columnType == null || !columnType.isNumeric()) {
    throw new QueryException(SQL_QUERY_UNSUPPORTED_ERROR_CODE, "...", null, null);
  }
  return WindowFrame.range(
      getBoundAsDouble(group.lowerBound),
      getBoundAsDouble(group.upperBound),
      new ColumnWithDirection(columnName, getOrdering().get(0).getDirection())
  );
}
```

[tool:write] {"content": "/*\n * Licensed to the Apache Software Foundation (ASF) under one\n * or more contributor license agreements.  See the NOTICE file\n * distributed with this work for additional information\n * regarding copyright ownership.  The ASF licenses this file\n * to you under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the\n * \"License\"); you may not use this file except in compliance\n * with the License.  You may obtain a copy of the License at\n *\n *   http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0\n *\n * Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing,\n * software distributed under the License is distributed on an\n * \"AS IS\" BASIS, WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY\n * KIND, either express or implied.  See the License for the\n * specific language governing permissions and limitations\n * under the License.\n */\n\npackage org.apache.druid.query.operator.window;\n\nimport com.google.common.collect.ImmutableList;\nimport org.apache.druid.error.DruidException;\nimport org.apache.druid.query.rowsandcols.RowsAndColumns;\nimport org.apache.druid.query.rowsandcols.column.Column;\nimport org.apache.druid.query.rowsandcols.column.ColumnAccessor;\nimport org.apache.druid.query.rowsandcols.semantic.ClusteredGroupPartitioner;\n\n/**\n * Resolves the frame boundaries of a value-based {@link WindowFrame.Range} frame.\n * <p>\n * The input must already be sorted, within each partition, by the ordering column of the frame.  Rows whose\n * ordering value is null are expected to be clustered at the start (ascending) or end (descending) of the\n * partition, as produced by the standard sort machinery.\n * <p>\n * Rows are grouped into peer groups (rows that share the same ordering value).  All rows of a peer group get\n * the same frame: the contiguous run of rows whose value falls between the current row's value plus the\n * lower offset and the current row's value plus the upper offset.  A row whose ordering value is null\n * resolves any finite bound to the corresponding edge of the null run.\n */\npublic class RangeFrameResolver\n{\n  /**\n   * Boundary positions of the peer groups; group {@code g} covers rows [{@code groupBoundaries[g]},\n   * {@code groupBoundaries[g + 1]}).\n   */\n  private final int[] groupBoundaries;\n  /**\n   * Frame start (inclusive) of each peer group.\n   */\n  private final int[] frameStarts;\n  /**\n   * Frame end (exclusive, never smaller than the frame start) of each peer group.\n   */\n  private final int[] frameEnds;\n\n  public RangeFrameResolver(final RowsAndColumns rac, final WindowFrame.Range frame)\n  {\n    final String orderColumn = frame.getOrderBy().getColumn();\n    final Column orderColumnObj = rac.findColumn(orderColumn);\n    if (orderColumnObj == null) {\n      throw DruidException.defensive(\n          \"Column [%s] specified in WindowFrame [%s] does not exist\",\n          orderColumn,\n          frame\n      );\n    }\n    final ColumnAccessor accessor = orderColumnObj.toAccessor();\n    final int numRows = accessor.numRows();\n\n    final boolean hasFiniteBound = frame.getLowerOffset() != null || frame.getUpperOffset() != null;\n    if (hasFiniteBound && !accessor.getType().isNumeric()) {\n      throw DruidException.defensive(\n          \"WindowFrame [%s] requires a numeric ordering column, found column [%s] of type [%s]\",\n          frame,\n          orderColumn,\n          accessor.getType()\n      );\n    }\n\n    this.groupBoundaries = ClusteredGroupPartitioner.fromRAC(rac).computeBoundaries(ImmutableList.of(orderColumn));\n    final int numGroups = groupBoundaries.length == 0 ? 0 : groupBoundaries.length - 1;\n    this.frameStarts = new int[numGroups];\n    this.frameEnds = new int[numGroups];\n\n    final boolean ascending = frame.getOrderBy().getDirection()\n                                                     .getDirectionInt() > 0;\n    final Double lowerOffset = frame.getLowerOffset();\n    final Double upperOffset = frame.getUpperOffset();\n\n    // Null values sort before all non-null values when ascending and after all non-null values when\n    // descending, so the null run is a prefix (ascending) or suffix (descending) of the partition.\n    final int nullRunStart;\n    final int nullRunEnd;\n    if (ascending) {\n      nullRunStart = 0;\n      nullRunEnd = countLeadingNulls(accessor, numRows);\n    } else {\n      nullRunEnd = numRows;\n      nullRunStart = numRows - countTrailingNulls(accessor, numRows);\n    }\n\n    for (int g = 0; g < numGroups; g++) {\n      final int groupStart = groupBoundaries[g];\n      if (accessor.isNull(groupStart)) {\n        // The current row's value is null; finite bounds resolve to the edges of the null run.\n        frameStarts[g] = lowerOffset == null ? 0 : nullRunStart;\n        frameEnds[g] = upperOffset == null ? numRows : nullRunEnd;\n        continue;\n      }\n\n      final double value = accessor.getDouble(groupStart);\n      if (ascending) {\n        frameStarts[g] = lowerOffset == null\n                        ? 0\n                        : lowerBoundAscending(accessor, nullRunEnd, numRows, value + lowerOffset);\n        frameEnds[g] = upperOffset == null\n                        ? numRows\n                        : upperBoundAscending(accessor, nullRunEnd, numRows, value + upperOffset);\n      } else {\n        frameStarts[g] = lowerOffset == null\n                        ? 0\n                        : lowerBoundDescending(accessor, 0, nullRunStart, value - lowerOffset);\n        frameEnds[g] = upperOffset == null\n                        ? numRows\n                        : upperBoundDescending(accessor, 0, nullRunStart, value - upperOffset);\n      }\n      // The frame can be empty (and the bounds can even be inverted) if the offset interval does not\n      // contain any value; normalize so that the frame end is never before the frame start.\n      frameEnds[g] = Math.max(frameStarts[g], frameEnds[g]);\n    }\n  }\n\n  public int getNumGroups()\n  {\n    return groupBoundaries.length == 0 ? 0 : groupBoundaries.length - 1;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * First row (inclusive) of peer group {@code group}.\n   */\n  public int getGroupStart(int group)\n  {\n    return groupBoundaries[group];\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * One past the last row (exclusive) of peer group {@code group}.\n   */\n  public int getGroupEnd(int group)\n  {\n    return groupBoundaries[group + 1];\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * First row (inclusive) of the frame of peer group {@code group}.\n   */\n  public int getFrameStart(int group)\n  {\n    return frameStarts[group];\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * One past the last row (exclusive) of the frame of peer group {@code group}.\n   */\n  public int getFrameEnd(int group)\n  {\n    return frameEnds[group];\n  }\n\n  private static int countLeadingNulls(final ColumnAccessor accessor, final int numRows)\n  {\n    int nulls = 0;\n    while (nulls < numRows && accessor.isNull(nulls)) {\n      nulls++;\n    }\n    return nulls;\n  }\n\n  private static int countTrailingNulls(final ColumnAccessor accessor, final int numRows)\n  {\n    int nulls = 0;\n    while (nulls < numRows && accessor.isNull(numRows - 1 - nulls)) {\n      nulls++;\n    }\n    return nulls;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * First row in [{@code lo}, {@code hi}) whose value is at least {@code target}, assuming the region is\n   * sorted ascending.  Returns {@code hi} if there is no such row.\n   */\n  private static int lowerBoundAscending(\n      final ColumnAccessor accessor,\n      final int lo,\n      final int hi,\n      final double target\n  )\n  {\n    int low = lo;\n    int high = hi;\n    while (low < high) {\n      final int mid = (low + high) >>> 1;\n      if (accessor.getDouble(mid) < target) {\n        low = mid + 1;\n      } else {\n        high = mid;\n      }\n    }\n    return low;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * First row in [{@code lo}, {@code hi}) whose value is greater than {@code target}, assuming the region is\n   * sorted ascending.  Returns {@code hi} if there is no such row.\n   */\n  private static int upperBoundAscending(\n      final ColumnAccessor accessor,\n      final int lo,\n      final int hi,\n      final double target\n  )\n  {\n    int low = lo;\n    int high = hi;\n    while (low < high) {\n      final int mid = (low + high) >>> 1;\n      if (accessor.getDouble(mid) <= target) {\n        low = mid + 1;\n      } else {\n        high = mid;\n      }\n    }\n    return low;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * First row in [{@code lo}, {@code hi}) whose value is at most {@code target}, assuming the region is\n   * sorted descending.  Returns {@code hi} if there is no such row.\n   */\n  private static int lowerBoundDescending(\n      final ColumnAccessor accessor,\n      final int lo,\n      final int hi,\n      final double target\n  )\n  {\n    int low = lo;\n    int high = hi;\n    while (low < high) {\n      final int mid = (low + high) >>> 1;\n      if (accessor.getDouble(mid) > target) {\n        low = mid + 1;\n      } else {\n        high = mid;\n      }\n    }\n    return low;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * First row in [{@code lo}, {@code hi}) whose value is less than {@code target}, assuming the region is\n   * sorted descending.  Returns {@code hi} if there is no such row.\n   */\n  private static int upperBoundDescending(\n      final ColumnAccessor accessor,\n      final int lo,\n      final int hi,\n      final double target\n  )\n  {\n    int low = lo;\n    int high = hi;\n    while (low < high) {\n      final int mid = (low + high) >>> 1;\n      if (accessor.getDouble(mid) >= target) {\n        low = mid + 1;\n      } else {\n        high = mid;\n      }\n    }\n    return low;\n  }\n}\n", "filePath": "/app/processing/src/main/java/org/apache/druid/query/operator/window/RangeFrameResolver.java"}

5. Extensive design reasoning in the conversation:
   - Range class design: implements WindowFrame directly (NOT extends OffsetFrame), with Double lowerOffset/upperOffset, ColumnWithDirection orderBy; @JsonCreator; equals/hashCode/toString; EqualsVerifier test.
   - No null checks in constructor (mirror Groups style) for EqualsVerifier friendliness.
   - RangeFrameResolver design: groupBoundaries, frameStarts, frameEnds arrays; getNumGroups, getGroupStart, getGroupEnd, getFrameStart, getFrameEnd.
   - Detailed null-handling semantics derivation:
     - Universal rule: null-key current row: lower finite → nullRunStart; upper finite → nullRunEnd; lower unbounded → 0; upper unbounded → numRows.
     - ASC: nulls first (null run = prefix [0, nullRunEnd)); DESC: nulls last (null run = [nullRunStart, numRows)).
     - Non-null row v ASC: frameStart = lower unbounded ? 0 : lowerBoundAsc(accessor, nullRunEnd, numRows, v + lower); frameEnd = upper unbounded ? numRows : upperBoundAsc(accessor, nullRunEnd, numRows, v + upper).
     - Non-null row v DESC: frameStart = lower unbounded ? 0 : lowerBoundDesc(accessor, 0, nullRunStart, v - lower); frameEnd = upper unbounded ? numRows : upperBoundDesc(accessor, 0, nullRunStart, v - upper).
     - Binary search helpers: lowerBoundAsc (first idx with getDouble ≥ target), upperBoundAsc (first idx > target), lowerBoundDesc (first idx ≤ target), upperBoundDesc (first idx < target).
     - Monotonicity: frameStart/frameEnd non-decreasing across groups in both directions; moveTo incremental branches safe (aggregators commutative).
     - Empty frame: frameStart > frameEnd → zero-row default; FIRST_VALUE/LAST_VALUE null on empty (results left untouched).
   - Validator change design: keep old message for unsupported; check orderList null or size != 1 → reject; isValidRangeBound checks SqlNumericLiteral or SqlIntervalLiteral with !isYearMonth.
   - Windowing.getWindowFrame: add isOffsetBound(bound) = bound.isPreceding() || bound.isFollowing(); RANGE branch → buildRangeFrame with defensive multi-key check, direction switch, sig.getColumnName/getColumnType, numeric type check; throw QueryException(SQL_QUERY_UNSUPPORTED...) pattern.
   - getBoundAsDouble: null for unbounded, 0.0 for CURRENT ROW, negate for PRECEDING, as-is for FOLLOWING, read via window.constants.get(refIndex - sig.size()).getValue() as Number doubleValue.
   - RexWindowBound.isPreceding()/isFollowing() semantics: only true for finite PRECEDING/FOLLOWING (UNBOUNDED PRECEDING kind is UNBOUNDED_PRECEDING ≠ PRECEDING) — the agent reasoned this.
   - Nulls direction constraints: validator rejects ASC NULLS LAST and DESC NULLS FIRST (lines ~769/775), so allowed combos match native null-first-ASC/null-last-DESC.
   - isEffectivelyUnbounded must handle Range BEFORE OffsetFrame unwrap (NPE risk): range with both offsets null → true → buildUnboundedIteratorFor.
   - buildRangeIteratorFor in DefaultFramedOnHeapAggregatable: new private static method returning Iterable<AggInterval> with AggInterval(Interval.of(groupStart, groupEnd), Interval.of(frameStart, frameEnd)).
   - WindowValueProcessorBase.computeFirstOrLastValueFramed Range branch: if frameStart < frameEnd, value = first ? accessor.getObject(frameStart) : accessor.getObject(frameEnd - 1); fill results for group rows; else leave null.
   - Resolver defensive checks: column missing → DruidException.defensive; type not numeric when any offset finite → DruidException.defensive (ColumnType.isNumeric() — need to verify exists).
   - Serde: WindowFramedAggregateProcessor holds WindowFrame field, serializes; ColumnWithDirection has @JsonCreator/@JsonProperty so serde works; register "range" in @JsonSubTypes.
   - Native users can construct WindowFrame.range directly via JSON in window operator queries.
   - testUnSupportedRangeBounds plan: change `ORDER BY dim1 RANGE` to `ORDER BY dim1, dim2 RANGE` → keeps same message AND same column 31 (since prefix `SELECT dim1,ROW_NUMBER() OVER (` unchanged; column 31 = the `(` after OVER). The agent verified column 31 = position of `(`.
   - ROW_NUMBER with string key + offset RANGE passes validator but ROW_NUMBER never calls getWindowFrame → silently succeeds; inconsistency accepted.
   - time_floor(__time,...) → TIMESTAMP → LONG → numeric → `ORDER BY time_floor(...) RANGE ...` works.
   - Calcite's own validator does NOT reject no-ORDER-BY RANGE offset before Druid's check (Druid's check runs first since validateWindow override runs checks before super).

## Objective
- Add native value-based RANGE window framing to Druid in /app: WindowFrame.Range type, resolver, native wiring (aggregation + value processors), SQL validator relaxation for single-key numeric/timestamp offset RANGE, planning in Windowing, plus tests.

## Important Details
Carry forward all the constraint bullets from prior summary (they're all still relevant — they're the requirements), plus new decisions from conversation:

Requirements (carry forward):
- WindowFrame.Range public nested class, Jackson subtype "range", range() factory, getLowerOffset/getUpperOffset/getOrderBy, null = unbounded, signed offsets, v+o ASC / v-o DESC, closed interval, unbounded sides reach partition edges including nulls, null-key finite bounds → null run edges, peers identical, empty frame → zero-row default, FIRST/LAST_VALUE null on empty, whole-partition Range ≡ unbounded(), LONG/FLOAT/DOUBLE work, timestamps arrive as LONG, non-numeric type + finite offset → unchecked DruidException, validator accepts offset RANGE (one-sided, CURRENT ROW), planning turns offset RANGE into native frames only with exactly one ORDER BY key numeric or timestamp, bound literal number or day-time INTERVAL → millis as double, year-month rejected, validation/planning rejection for non-numeric non-interval keys.

Prior decisions (carry forward):
- RangeFrameResolver in org.apache.druid.query.operator.window, constructor (RowsAndColumns, WindowFrame.Range), groupBoundaries/frameStarts/frameEnds, getNumGroups/getGroupStart/getGroupEnd/getFrameStart/getFrameEnd.
- computeBoundaries on ORDER BY column for peer groups.
- No merging adjacent identical-frame groups.
- getDouble for key comparison, precision loss beyond 2^53 accepted.
- isEffectivelyUnbounded: both offsets null → unbounded.
- moveTo recomputes whole interval for Range.
- Search covers only non-null rows.
- ASC frame start = first row ≥ lower bound; ASC frame end = first row > upper bound exclusive; DESC frame start = first row ≤ lower bound; DESC frame end = first row < upper bound exclusive.
- Validator rejection message kept: "Order By with RANGE clause currently supports only UNBOUNDED or CURRENT ROW. Use ROWS clause instead."
- qaWin files left as recorded.
- No-ORDER-BY offset RANGE rejected.
- Exactly one ORDER BY key.
- Bound literal numeric or day-time INTERVAL only.
- Key type check at planning time in Windowing.getWindowFrame (sig available).
- isLongType counts TIMESTAMP as long → sig reports timestamp keys as LONG.
- getWindowFrame keeps groups(...) path when no PRECEDING/FOLLOWING offset; emits range(...) only when at least one bound is PRECEDING/FOLLOWING offset.
- getBoundAsDouble: null unbounded, 0.0 CURRENT ROW, negated PRECEDING, as-is FOLLOWING, read via window.constants.get(refIndex - sig.size()).getValue() Number doubleValue.
- Year-month INTERVAL bounds rejected.
- testUnSupportedRangeBounds currently runs ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY dim1 RANGE BETWEEN 3 PRECEDING AND 2 FOLLOWING), dim1 STRING, expects old message.
- EqualsVerifier usingGetClass pattern.

New from conversation:
- Confirmed every qaWin RANGE case has no ORDER BY or ≥2 keys (3 or 4), none single-key → validator rule keeps all recorded QueryInterruptedException intact. This resolves the "unconfirmed" item.
- qaWin case examples: 3-key `OVER (PARTITION BY client_ip ORDER BY server_ip, time_floor(__time, 'PT12H') ASC, timezone RANGE 0 FOLLOWING)` etc., 4-key cases, and no-ORDER-BY `OVER (PARTITION BY client_ip RANGE 0 FOLLOWING)`.
- Range must NOT extend OffsetFrame (already in prior); Range implements WindowFrame directly; constructor mirrors Groups style (no null checks) for EqualsVerifier friendliness.
- Null-run detection: sort guarantees nulls contiguous; ASC nulls = prefix [0, nullRunEnd), DESC nulls = suffix [nullRunStart, numRows); use accessor.isNull scan rather than compareRows null semantics for null run location.
- Universal null-key rule: lower finite → nullRunStart; upper finite → nullRunEnd; lower unbounded → 0; upper unbounded → numRows. (ASC nullRunStart=0; DESC nullRunEnd=numRows so rule is uniform.)
- Null-row frame with any finite bounds = null run (DuckDB-like semantics).
- Binary search helpers: lowerBoundAsc/upperBoundAsc/lowerBoundDesc/upperBoundDesc over non-null region.
- Monotonic: frameStart/frameEnd non-decreasing per group in both directions; moveTo incremental branches remain correct (commutative aggregators).
- RexWindowBound.isPreceding()/isFollowing() true only for finite PRECEDING/FOLLOWING (UNBOUNDED_PRECEDING is a separate SqlKind) → isOffsetBound helper.
- Validator rejects ASC NULLS LAST and DESC NULLS FIRST → allowed nulls direction matches native sort semantics.
- isValidRangeBound: isUnboundedOrCurrent → true; PRECEDING/FOLLOWING operand SqlNumericLiteral → true; SqlIntervalLiteral with !IntervalValue.getIntervalQualifier().isYearMonth() → true; else false.
- orderList null or size != 1 → old message.
- buildRangeFrame: defensive multi-key check unreachable via SQL but kept; direction switch; sig.getColumnName/getColumnType(fieldIndex); non-numeric → QueryException SQL_QUERY_UNSUPPORTED pattern.
- isEffectivelyUnbounded must branch on Range BEFORE OffsetFrame unwrap (NPE risk).

- WindowValueProcessorBase Range branch: frameStart < frameEnd → first ? getObject(frameStart) : getObject(frameEnd-1); fill results[groupStart..groupEnd); empty → leave null.

Let me finalize. Keep the Important Details comprehensive but terse. This is a lot of bullets but they're all needed context.
## Objective
- Add native value-based `RANGE` window framing to Druid in `/app`.
- The new type must be `WindowFrame.Range`.
- `WindowFrame.Range` must be a public nested class of `WindowFrame`.
- `WindowFrame.Range` must be registered as Jackson subtype `"range"`.
- Multi-key `RANGE` queries must still fail when the work is done.

## Important Details
- `WindowFrame.Range` must have a `@Nullable Double lowerOffset`.
- `WindowFrame.Range` must have a `@Nullable Double upperOffset`.
- `WindowFrame.Range` must have a non-null `ColumnWithDirection orderBy`.
- The required getter for the lower offset is `getLowerOffset()`.
- The required getter for the upper offset is `getUpperOffset()`.
- The required getter for the ordering column is `getOrderBy()`.
- A null offset must mean a positionally unbounded side.
- A non-null offset must be a signed distance in the ordering key's value space.
- The required static factory is `WindowFrame.range(@Nullable Double lowerOffset, @Nullable Double upperOffset, ColumnWithDirection orderBy)`.
- `Range` must not extend `OffsetFrame`.
- `Range` must override `equals` by value.
- `Range` must override `hashCode` by value.
- `Range` must support Jackson serialization.
- `Range` must support Jackson deserialization.
- `FramedOnHeapAggregatable#aggregateAll` must accept `Range`.
- `WindowFramedAggregateProcessor` must accept `Range`.
- `WindowFirstProcessor` must accept `Range`.
- `WindowLastProcessor` must accept `Range`.
- For a non-null ordering key value `v` and a finite offset `o`, a bound must be `v + o` under `ASC`.
- For a non-null ordering key value `v` and a finite offset `o`, a bound must be `v - o` under `DESC`.
- A `Range` frame must be the closed interval between its lower bound and its upper bound.
- Unbounded sides of a `Range` frame must reach the partition edges.
- Unbounded sides of a `Range` frame must include rows whose key is `NULL`.
- When the current row's key is `NULL`, finite bounds must resolve to the edges of the contiguous `NULL` run that holds the row.
- Peers with equal keys must get identical frame bounds.
- Peers with equal keys must get identical output.
- An empty frame must yield the aggregator's zero-row default.
- `FIRST_VALUE` must return `null` on an empty frame.
- `LAST_VALUE` must return `null` on an empty frame.
- A `Range` that covers the whole partition for every row must evaluate the same as `WindowFrame.unbounded()`.
- `LONG` ordering columns must work.
- `FLOAT` ordering columns must work.
- `DOUBLE` ordering columns must work.
- Range resolution must work for every `RowsAndColumns` implementation.
- Timestamp columns reach the native engine as `LONG`.
- An ordering type that cannot do value arithmetic with a finite offset must throw an unchecked `DruidException`.
- The SQL validator must accept offset `RANGE` frames.
- The SQL validator must accept one-sided offset `RANGE` frames.
- The SQL validator must accept offset `RANGE` frames with a `CURRENT ROW` bound.
- Calcite planning must turn offset `RANGE` frames into native frames.
- Offset `RANGE` support must apply only when the window has exactly one `ORDER BY` key.
- The single key must be numeric or a timestamp.
- The bound literal may be a number.
- The bound literal may be a day-time `INTERVAL`.
- A day-time `INTERVAL` must map to its millisecond magnitude as a floating-point offset with no integer truncation.
- An offset `RANGE` frame with finite bounds on a non-numeric and non-interval key must be rejected during validation or planning.
- `WindowFrame` maps the Jackson name `"rows"` to `WindowFrame.Rows`.
- `WindowFrame` maps the Jackson name `"groups"` to `WindowFrame.Groups`.
- `WindowFrame.unbounded()` returns `rows(null, null)`.
- The agent found earlier that `ColumnWithDirection` encodes `ASC` as `ASC(1)`, read through `getDirectionInt()`.
- The agent found earlier that `ColumnWithDirection` encodes `DESC` as `DESC(-1)`, read through `getDirectionInt()`.
- The agent found earlier that only `DefaultFramedOnHeapAggregatable` implements `FramedOnHeapAggregatable` in the main code.
- The agent concluded that handling `Range` in `DefaultFramedOnHeapAggregatable` and in the value processors covers every `RowsAndColumns`.
- Decision: `WindowFrame.Range` will implement `WindowFrame` directly.
- The agent judged that `OffsetFrame` has `Integer` offsets.
- Decision: the planned `Range` constructor will not null-check `orderBy`.
- This replaces an earlier draft with a null check, because the agent judged that `EqualsVerifier` may reject one.
- The agent judged that `compareRows` returns -1 for a null left row, so null keys sort first under `ASC`.
- The agent judged that the sort multiplies `compareRows` by the direction, so null keys sort last under `DESC`.
- The agent judged that `compareRows(null, null)` returns 0, so the `NULL` rows form one peer group.
- Decision: a new class `RangeFrameResolver` in `org.apache.druid.query.operator.window` will compute the peer groups and the frame start and end of each group.
- The reason is that `DefaultFramedOnHeapAggregatable` and `WindowValueProcessorBase.computeFirstOrLastValueFramed` both need the peer groups and their frame intervals.
- Decision: the peer groups come from `computeBoundaries` on the `ORDER BY` column.
- The planned `RangeFrameResolver` constructor takes `RowsAndColumns rac` and `WindowFrame.Range frame`.
- The planned `RangeFrameResolver` keeps the peer group edges in `groupBoundaries`.
- The planned `RangeFrameResolver` keeps one frame start per group in `frameStarts`.
- The planned `RangeFrameResolver` keeps one frame end per group in `frameEnds`.
- Decision: both users of `RangeFrameResolver` will iterate the peer groups directly, without merging adjacent groups that have identical frames.
- The agent judged that such merging is possible but not worth doing.
- Decision: in `computeFirstOrLastValueFramed`, a `Range` peer group's `FIRST_VALUE` takes `getObject(frameStart)`.
- Decision: in `computeFirstOrLastValueFramed`, a `Range` peer group's `LAST_VALUE` takes `getObject(frameEnd - 1)`.
- Decision: a `Range` peer group gets a null value unless `frameStart < frameEnd`.
- Decision: `RangeFrameResolver` will throw a defensive `DruidException` when an offset is finite and the `ORDER BY` column is not numeric.
- Decision: `RangeFrameResolver` will throw `DruidException.defensive("Column[%s] does not exist", orderColumn)` when the `ORDER BY` column is missing.
- Decision: keys are compared as doubles through `accessor.getDouble(row)`.
- The reason is that `getDouble` treats `LONG`, `FLOAT` and `DOUBLE` keys the same way.
- The agent accepted a precision loss for key values beyond 2^53.
- Under `ASC`, the planned frame start is the first row whose value is at least the lower bound.
- Under `ASC`, the planned frame end is the first row whose value is greater than the upper bound, exclusive.
- Under `DESC`, the planned frame start is the first row whose value is at most the lower bound.
- Under `DESC`, the planned frame end is the first row whose value is less than the upper bound, exclusive.

Note the `moveTo` concern I worked out earlier: frameStart/frameEnd are monotone non-decreasing across groups, and I normalize frameEnd = max(frameStart, frameEnd) in the resolver, so `Interval(fs, fe)` always has fs ≤ fe. The moveTo incremental branches:

- Branch 1: currentRows.a == newRows.a && currentRows.b < newRows.b → adds rows [currentRows.b, newRows.b). For this to be correct, aggregators must currently contain [newRows.a, currentRows.b) and we extend to [newRows.a, newRows.b). Since all previous aggregations covered prefix [newRows.a, currentRows.b)? Hmm — aggregators contain the union of everything aggregated so far in this aggregator instance. The aggregator instance was created (newAggregators) at some point and rows aggregated in order.

Wait — actually the correctness of moveTo's incremental branches relies on the aggregators being commutative and containing exactly the union of rows aggregated since last newAggregators. If current interval was [a, b) and new is [a, b') with b' > b, aggregators contain {rows in [a,b)} → aggregate [b, b') → now contain [a, b') ✓ correct IF aggregators contain exactly [a,b). They do if the previous moveTo call(s) since newAggregators resulted in exactly [a, b) — by induction, each moveTo leaves currentRows = the exact set aggregated IF the set is always an interval and updates only extend at one end. For monotone frames: consecutive groups have fs non-decreasing and fe non-decreasing. From [fs1, fe1) to [fs2, fe2): if fs2 == fs1 and fe2 > fe1 → branch 1 ✓ exact. If fs2 > fs1 and fe2 == fe1 → branch 2: aggregate [fs1, fs2)?? wait branch 2: currentRows.a > newRows.a && currentRows.b == newRows.b → aggregate [newRows.a, currentRows.a) = [fs2, fs1)... hold on:

Correct: aggregators accumulate, so adding earlier rows extends the set downward. Yes ✓.

```java
  public static Iterable<AggInterval> buildIteratorFor(AppendableRowsAndColumns rac, WindowFrame frame)
  {
    int numRows = rac.numRows();
    if (isEffectivelyUnbounded(frame, numRows)) {
      return buildUnboundedIteratorFor(rac);
    }
    Rows rowsFrame = frame.unwrap(WindowFrame.Rows.class);
    if (rowsFrame != null) {
      return buildRowIteratorFor(rac, rowsFrame);
    }
    Groups groupsFrame = frame.unwrap(WindowFrame.Groups.class);
    if (groupsFrame != null) {
      return buildGroupIteratorFor(rac, groupsFrame);
    }
    WindowFrame.Range rangeFrame = frame.unwrap(WindowFrame.Range.class);
    if (rangeFrame != null) {
      return buildRangeIteratorFor(rac, rangeFrame);
    }
    throw DruidException.defensive("Unable to handle WindowFrame [%s]!", frame);
  }

    if (rangeFrame != null) {

    }
    OffsetFrame offsetFrame = frame.unwrap(WindowFrame.OffsetFrame.class);

  }
```

Now buildRangeIteratorFor:

```java
  private static Iterable<AggInterval> buildRangeIteratorFor(AppendableRowsAndColumns rac, WindowFrame.Range frame)
  {
    final RangeFrameResolver resolver = new RangeFrameResolver(rac, frame);
    final int numGroups = resolver.getNumGroups();
    return () -> new Iterator<AggInterval>()
    {
      int currentGroup = 0;

      @Override
      public boolean hasNext()
      {
        return currentGroup < numGroups;
      }

      @Override
      public AggInterval next()
      {
        if (!hasNext()) {
          throw new IllegalStateException();
        }
        final int g = currentGroup++;
        return new AggInterval(
            Interval.of(resolver.getGroupStart(g), resolver.getGroupEnd(g)),
            Interval.of(resolver.getFrameStart(g), resolver.getFrameEnd(g))
        );
      }
    };
  }
```

Import RangeFrameResolver: org.apache.druid.query.operator.window.RangeFrameResolver. There's already `import org.apache.druid.query.operator.window.WindowFrame;` etc. Add import.

Wait — empty rac (numRows == 0): resolver numGroups = 0 → iterator empty → resultRac writes nothing → results arrays [numRows=0] → fine ✓. Hmm — but aggregateAll would produce zero rows... but partitions are non-empty in practice; WindowFirstProcessor throws defensive on empty partitions. OK.

Edge: `RANGE UNBOUNDED PRECEDING AND UNBOUNDED FOLLOWING` → isEffectivelyUnbounded → buildUnboundedIteratorFor ✓ (one group covering all rows, frame = all rows).

Now I'll edit `getWindowFrame` in Windowing.java:

```java
    public WindowFrame getWindowFrame()
    {
      if (group.lowerBound.isUnbounded() && group.upperBound.isUnbounded()) {
        return WindowFrame.unbounded();
      }
      if (group.isRows) {
        return WindowFrame.rows(getBoundAsInteger(group.lowerBound), getBoundAsInteger(group.upperBound));
      }
      if (isOffsetBound(group.lowerBound) || isOffsetBound(group.upperBound)) {
        /* Value-based RANGE frame; the SQL validator only accepts these with a single numeric or timestamp
         * ordering key, but check again here since native queries can reach this point too.
         */
        return buildRangeFrame();
      } else {
        /* Right now we support GROUPS based framing in the native layer;
         * but the SQL layer doesn't accept that as of now.
         */
        return WindowFrame.groups(getBoundAsInteger(group.lowerBound), getBoundAsInteger(group.upperBound), getOrderingColumNames());
      }
    }
```

Wait — hmm, `getWindowFrame` is only called from Windowing (SQL layer). Native queries construct WindowFrame directly via JSON. But defensive checks are still good (as decided: "defensive multi-key check unreachable via SQL but kept").

Wait — one important check: `WindowFrame.groups(...)` with getOrderingColumNames() — for the RANGE-without-offsets case (UNBOUNDED/CURRENT ROW bounds only). Now `buildRangeFrame` needs `sig.getColumnType(fieldIndex)`. RowSignature.getColumnType(int) returns Optional<ColumnType>. Let me double check what `sig.getColumnName(fieldIndex)` is — already used in getOrdering ✓.

```java
    private static boolean isOffsetBound(RexWindowBound bound)

    }
```


Also getConstant returns int — I need a double version:

```java
    private Double getBoundAsDouble(RexWindowBound bound)
    {
      if (bound.isUnbounded()) {
        return null;
      }
      if (bound.isCurrentRow()) {
        return 0.0d;
      }
      final double value = getConstant(((RexInputRef) bound.getOffset()).getIndex());
      return bound.isPreceding() ? -value : value;
    }
```

Wait — getConstant returns int... `getConstant(...)` returns int → assign to double fine. But the constant might be a huge long (interval millis beyond int range!) — getConstant does `.intValue()` — OVERFLOW! Must NOT use getConstant for range; need raw:

```java
    private double getConstantAsDouble(int refIndex)
    {
      return ((Number) window.constants.get(refIndex - sig.size()).getValue()).doubleValue();
    }
```

And getBoundAsDouble uses that.

And buildRangeFrame:

```java
    private WindowFrame buildRangeFrame()
    {
      final List<RelFieldCollation> fields = group.orderKeys.getFieldCollations();
      if (fields.size() != 1) {
        throw new QueryException(
            QueryException.SQL_QUERY_UNSUPPORTED_ERROR_CODE,
            StringUtils.format(
                "Order By with RANGE clause with offset-based bounds requires exactly one ordering key, found [%d]",
                fields.size()
            ),
            null,
            null
        );
      }
      final RelFieldCollation field = fields.get(0);
      final String columnName = sig.getColumnName(field.getFieldIndex());
      final ColumnType columnType = sig.getColumnType(field.getFieldIndex()).orElse(null);
      if (columnType == null || !columnType.isNumeric()) {
        throw new QueryException(
            QueryException.SQL_QUERY_UNSUPPORTED_ERROR_CODE,
            StringUtils.format(
                "Order By with RANGE clause with offset-based bounds requires a numeric or timestamp ordering key, found column [%s] of type [%s]",
                columnName,
                columnType == null ? "UNKNOWN" : columnType
            ),
            null,
            null
        );
      }
      return WindowFrame.range(
          getBoundAsDouble(group.lowerBound),
          getBoundAsDouble(group.upperBound),
          new ColumnWithDirection(columnName, getOrdering().get(0).getDirection())
      );
    }
```

Hmm wait — `sig.getColumnType(field.getFieldIndex())` — RowSignature has `getColumnType(final int columnNumber)` returning Optional<ColumnType> ✓ (I saw `public Optional<ColumnType> getColumnType(final int columnNumber)` at line 136).

Wait, hold on — do I need `.get(0)` from getOrdering()? getOrdering() builds ColumnWithDirection for ALL fields — but we already verified fields.size() == 1 → get(0) is the same field ✓ direction ✓. But cleaner:

```java
final ColumnWithDirection.Direction direction = getOrdering().get(0).getDirection();
```

Hmm — or inline the direction switch. Reuse getOrdering() simpler ✓.

```java
    if (!targetWindow.isRows() &&
        (!isUnboundedOrCurrent(lowerBound) || !isUnboundedOrCurrent(upperBound))) {
      // OFFSET PRECEDING/FOLLOWING RANGE frames are supported when the ORDER BY has exactly one key
      // and the bounds are numeric or day-time interval literals.

      final boolean validRange =
          orderList != null && orderList.size() == 1
          && isValidRangeBound(lowerBound) && isValidRangeBound(upperBound);

```

Hmm wait — orderList.size() == 1 — but `ORDER BY x` where x is an expression... size() counts order items (expressions), not keys. "ORDER BY x, y" → size 2 ✓. "ORDER BY x" → size 1 ✓. That's what we want (exactly one ORDER BY key).

Hmm — wait, but SqlWindow.isRows(): is it the raw `isRows` flag? `RANGE` → isRows false ✓. 

Now isValidRangeBound:

```java
  /**
   * Checks if the given RANGE window frame bound is supported: it must be UNBOUNDED, CURRENT ROW, or a
   * PRECEDING/FOLLOWING offset that is a numeric or day-time interval literal.
   */
  private boolean isValidRangeBound(@Nullable SqlNode bound)
  {
    if (isUnboundedOrCurrent(bound)) {
      return true;
    }
    if (bound.getKind() == SqlKind.FOLLOWING || bound.getKind() == SqlKind.PRECEDING) {
      final SqlNode boundVal = ((SqlCall) bound).operand(0);
      if (boundVal instanceof SqlNumericLiteral) {
        return true;
      }
      if (boundVal instanceof SqlIntervalLiteral
          && !((SqlIntervalLiteral.IntervalValue) ((SqlIntervalLiteral) boundVal).getValue())
              .getIntervalQualifier().isYearMonth()) {
        return true;
      }
    }
    return false;
  }
```

Wait — SqlIntervalLiteral.IntervalValue: `getIntervalQualifier()` returns SqlIntervalQualifier, `.isYearMonth()` — exists ✓. Day-time → false → supported ✓.

Hmm — but what about negative numeric literals? `RANGE BETWEEN -3 PRECEDING`... hmm, wait can you even write negative offsets in SQL? `BETWEEN 3 PRECEDING AND 2 FOLLOWING` — the literal 3 with PRECEDING. A negative literal `BETWEEN -3 PRECEDING` — Calcite parses `SqlLiteral(-3)`? Hmm — `-3` is unary minus operator applied to 3 → SqlNumericLiteral with value -3 ✓ still SqlNumericLiteral. Fine — the resolver handles sign math generically (v + lower where lower = -(−3)... wait: getBoundAsDouble negates for preceding: value = -3 → -(-3) = +3?? Hmm: PRECEDING with value -3 → semantically "3 FOLLOWING"?? In Calcite's updateBoundsIfNeeded/RexWindowBound: `RANGE BETWEEN -3 PRECEDING` — hmm, the RexWindowBound for `-3 PRECEDING`... offset = -3, isPreceding → in RexWindowBound, the offset value is stored as the literal value; getBoundAsInteger negates it for preceding. Whatever Calcite's standard semantics is, both ROWS and RANGE paths use the same getBoundAsDouble/getBoundAsInteger transform, so RANGE is consistent with ROWS ✓. Postgres/standard: " -3 PRECEDING" isn't legal I think (requires non-negative). Don't care.

Also — the check `orderList != null` — getOrderList() returns SqlNodeList, might be null when no ORDER BY? In Calcite, SqlWindow always has an orderList (possibly empty). qaWin `OVER (PARTITION BY client_ip RANGE 0 FOLLOWING)` — orderList empty → size() == 0 ≠ 1 → rejected ✓ old message ✓.

Wait — hmm, actually, will the qaWin no-ORDER-BY case even reach my check? Yes: !isRows ✓, bounds `RANGE 0 FOLLOWING` → not unbounded/current ✓ → check: orderList size 0 → reject ✓ good, same error as before (before it also rejected since 0 FOLLOWING is not unbounded/current) ✓.
